prose-gray dark:prose-invert [contain:inline-size] isolate empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Modifying system prompts" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/modifying-system-prompts" id="content"><span data-as="p">System prompts define Claude’s behavior, capabilities, and response style. Start from the <code>claude_code</code> preset for CLI or IDE-like coding tools where a human watches and steers the work. Write your own prompt for agents with a different surface, identity, or permission model.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="how-system-prompts-work" aria-labelledby="_R_39deehkd74t9_"><a href="#how-system-prompts-work" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: How system prompts work"></a><span id="_R_39deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">How system prompts work</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">A system prompt is the initial instruction set that shapes how Claude behaves throughout a conversation. The Agent SDK has three starting points for it:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Minimal default</strong>: when you don’t set <code>systemPrompt</code> in TypeScript or <code>system_prompt</code> in Python, the SDK uses a minimal prompt that covers tool calling but omits the rest of the <code>claude_code</code> preset’s content, including its security and safety instructions. This differs from <code>claude -p</code>, which uses the Claude Code system prompt by default. If you’re migrating from the CLI and want matching behavior, set the <code>claude_code</code> preset.</li>
<li><strong><code>claude_code</code> preset</strong>: the system prompt that the Claude Code CLI uses, with tool usage instructions and security and safety instructions. Set <code>systemPrompt: { type: &quot;preset&quot;, preset: &quot;claude_code&quot; }</code> in TypeScript or <code>system_prompt={&quot;type&quot;: &quot;preset&quot;, &quot;preset&quot;: &quot;claude_code&quot;}</code> in Python, optionally with <code>append</code> to add your own instructions on the end.</li>
<li><strong>Custom string</strong>: a prompt you write yourself. The SDK sends only what you provide.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="decide-on-a-starting-point" aria-labelledby="_R_99deehkd74t9_"><a href="#decide-on-a-starting-point" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Decide on a starting point"></a><span id="_R_99deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Decide on a starting point</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The deciding factor is how closely your agent resembles Claude Code: a coding agent operating in a repository, with a human watching streaming output and steering the work. The further your product is from that, the more you’ll want to write your own prompt.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_d9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">You’re building</th><th style="text-align:left">Use</th><th style="text-align:left">What you get</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left">A CLI or IDE-like coding tool where a human watches and steers, and Claude Code’s defaults are what you want</td><td style="text-align:left"><code>claude_code</code> preset</td><td style="text-align:left">The Claude Code prompt, including tool guidance and safety rules</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">The same kind of tool, plus product-specific rules like coding standards, output format, or domain context</td><td style="text-align:left"><code>claude_code</code> preset with <code>append</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Everything above, with your instructions added after the preset. Nothing is removed, so this is the lowest-risk customization</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">An agent with a different surface, identity, or permission model, or a non-coding agent</td><td style="text-align:left">Custom prompt string</td><td style="text-align:left">Only what you write. You take responsibility for replacing the tool guidance and safety instructions your agent still needs</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">A thin tool-calling loop with no agent persona, where you supply all behavior in the user prompt</td><td style="text-align:left">No <code>systemPrompt</code> option</td><td style="text-align:left">The minimal default: tool-calling support and nothing else</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">“Different from Claude Code” usually means one of the following:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Different surface</strong>: the output isn’t read in a terminal by the person who triggered it. Chat UIs, structured-output consumers, and non-coding automation each need a prompt that matches how their output is rendered and reviewed. Unattended coding automation, like a CI job that fixes lint errors or reviews diffs, still fits the preset because the work itself is what the preset is written for.</li>
<li><strong>Different identity</strong>: the agent shouldn’t present itself as Claude Code. A support bot, a data-analysis assistant, or any domain-specific agent needs its own name, scope, and persona.</li>
<li><strong>Different permission model</strong>: the agent runs autonomously without a human approving each step, or operates on a narrow set of resources. Claude Code’s prompt assumes a human is in the loop with access to a full toolset.</li>
<li><strong>Non-coding tasks</strong>: most of Claude Code’s prompt is coding guidance. For research, content, or operations agents, that guidance competes with the instructions you actually need.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The <a href="#compare-the-four-approaches" class="link">comparison table</a> shows what each customization method preserves.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="customize-agent-behavior" aria-labelledby="_R_l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#customize-agent-behavior" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Customize agent behavior"></a><span id="_R_l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Customize agent behavior</span></h2>
<span data-as="p"><code>append</code> and a custom prompt string each change the system prompt directly, and an output style changes the instructions Claude Code gives Claude for every response. CLAUDE.md takes a different path: the SDK reads it and injects its content into the conversation as project context, so it shapes behavior alongside whichever system prompt you choose. <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/skills" class="link">Skills</a>, <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">hooks</a>, and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">permissions</a> also shape behavior outside the system prompt and are covered on their own pages.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="claude-md-files-for-project-level-instructions" aria-labelledby="_R_p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#claude-md-files-for-project-level-instructions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: CLAUDE.md files for project-level instructions"></a><span id="_R_p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">CLAUDE.md files for project-level instructions</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">CLAUDE.md files give Claude persistent project context and instructions. The SDK injects their content into the conversation and leaves the system prompt untouched, so they work with any system prompt configuration. For what to put in CLAUDE.md, where to place it, and how to write effective instructions, see <a href="/docs/en/memory#when-to-add-to-claude-md" class="link">When to add to CLAUDE.md</a> and the rest of <a href="/docs/en/memory" class="link">How Claude remembers your project</a>. This section covers what’s specific to the SDK: how CLAUDE.md loads.</span>
<span data-as="p">The SDK reads CLAUDE.md when the matching setting source is enabled: <code>&#x27;project&#x27;</code> loads <code>CLAUDE.md</code> or <code>.claude/CLAUDE.md</code> from the working directory, and <code>&#x27;user&#x27;</code> loads <code>~/.claude/CLAUDE.md</code>. Default <code>query()</code> options enable both sources, so CLAUDE.md loads automatically. If you set <code>settingSources</code> in TypeScript or <code>setting_sources</code> in Python explicitly, include the sources you need. CLAUDE.md loading is controlled by setting sources, not by the <code>claude_code</code> preset.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="load-claude-md-with-the-sdk" aria-labelledby="_R_v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#load-claude-md-with-the-sdk" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Load CLAUDE.md with the SDK"></a><span id="_R_v9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Load CLAUDE.md with the SDK</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">To load CLAUDE.md, set <code>settingSources</code> to include the level where you keep your CLAUDE.md. The example below loads a project-level CLAUDE.md alongside the <code>claude_code</code> preset, so Claude has both the coding-agent prompt and your project’s conventions:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <span data-as="p">When you run either example, the SDK streams messages as Claude works: a system init message, assistant messages, user messages carrying tool results, and a final result message with the session outcome.</span>
<span data-as="p">CLAUDE.md is persistent across all sessions in a project, shared with your team through git, and discovered automatically without code changes. It is not loaded if you pass an empty <code>settingSources</code> array.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="output-styles-for-persistent-configurations" aria-labelledby="_R_199deehkd74t9_"><a href="#output-styles-for-persistent-configurations" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Output styles for persistent configurations"></a><span id="_R_199deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Output styles for persistent configurations</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Output styles are saved sets of instructions that change Claude’s role, tone, and output format. They’re stored as markdown files and can be reused across sessions and projects.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="create-an-output-style" aria-labelledby="_R_1d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#create-an-output-style" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Create an output style"></a><span id="_R_1d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Create an output style</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">An output style is a markdown file with <a href="/docs/en/output-styles#frontmatter" class="link">frontmatter</a> for metadata, followed by the prompt content. Save it to <code>~/.claude/output-styles/</code> for a user-level style available in every project, or <code>.claude/output-styles/</code> in your repository for a project-level style you can commit and share with your team.</span>
<span data-as="p">A custom output style leaves the <code>claude_code</code> preset’s software engineering instructions out and uses your own. To keep them and layer your instructions on top, set <code>keep-coding-instructions: true</code> in the frontmatter. Those instructions are only in Claude Code’s full system prompt, so the setting has no effect in a session on the shorter system prompt; set <a href="/docs/en/env-vars#variables" class="link"><code>CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT</code></a> to <code>0</code> to select the full prompt on any model. Keep them when your agent is still doing software engineering work. Leave them out when you’re replacing the role entirely.</span>
<span data-as="p">The example below defines a code-review persona that keeps the coding instructions, since reviewing code still benefits from Claude Code’s security and code-quality guidance. Save it as <code>~/.claude/output-styles/code-reviewer.md</code> to make it available across projects:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/5 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">---</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#116329;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">name</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">: </span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">Code Reviewer</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#116329;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">keep-coding-instructions</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">: </span><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">true</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">---</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">You are an expert code reviewer.</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">For every code submission:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#6796E6">1.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> Check for bugs and security issues</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#6796E6">2.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> Evaluate performance</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#6796E6">4.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> Rate code quality (1-10)</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="activate-an-output-style" aria-labelledby="_R_1n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#activate-an-output-style" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Activate an output style"></a><span id="_R_1n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Activate an output style</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Once created, activate output styles via:</span>
<ul>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong>CLI</strong>: run <code>/output-style &lt;style&gt;</code>, for example <code>/output-style concise</code>, or run <code>/config</code> and select one. The <code>/output-style</code> command requires Claude Code v2.1.269 or later.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Settings</strong>: set <code>outputStyle</code> in <code>.claude/settings.local.json</code></span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong>TypeScript SDK</strong>: set <code>outputStyle</code> inside the inline <code>settings</code> object passed to <code>query()</code>, or point <code>settings</code> at a settings file that sets it. <code>outputStyle</code> is not a top-level <code>Options</code> field:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <span data-as="p">In the Python SDK, set <code>outputStyle</code> through the <code>settings</code> option, which takes a JSON string such as <code>&#x27;{&quot;outputStyle&quot;: &quot;Explanatory&quot;}&#x27;</code> or a path to a settings file that sets it.</span>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Note for SDK users:</strong> Output styles are loaded when you include <code>settingSources: [&#x27;user&#x27;]</code> or <code>settingSources: [&#x27;project&#x27;]</code> (TypeScript) / <code>setting_sources=[&quot;user&quot;]</code> or <code>setting_sources=[&quot;project&quot;]</code> (Python) in your options.</span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">            }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">        ),</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">    ):</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">        messages.append(message)</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0">        if</span><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA"> isinstance</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(message, AssistantMessage):</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">            print</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(message.content)</span></span>

<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">asyncio.run(main())</span></span>

<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="improve-prompt-caching-across-users-and-machines" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#improve-prompt-caching-across-users-and-machines" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Improve prompt caching across users and machines"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Improve prompt caching across users and machines</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">By default, two sessions that use the same <code>claude_code</code> preset and <code>append</code> text still can’t share a prompt cache entry when their auto memory locations differ. The preset embeds that location in the system prompt ahead of your <code>append</code> text. The location defaults to an absolute path under <code>~/.claude/projects/</code> named for the repository’s path on disk, so it differs across users, machines, and checkouts.</span>
<span data-as="p">CLAUDE.md content and environment details such as the working directory, platform, shell, and OS version don’t affect the system prompt cache, because Claude Code delivers them in the conversation, not the system prompt.</span>
<span data-as="p">To make the system prompt identical across sessions, set <code>excludeDynamicSections: true</code> in TypeScript or <code>&quot;exclude_dynamic_sections&quot;: True</code> in Python. The per-user context moves into the first user message, leaving only the static preset and your <code>append</code> text in the system prompt so identical configurations share a cache entry across users and machines.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><code>excludeDynamicSections</code> requires <code>@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk</code> v0.2.98 or later, or <code>claude-agent-sdk</code> v0.1.58 or later for Python. Set it on the preset object form only. The SDK ignores it when you pass a custom prompt instead of the preset; to keep a custom prompt’s instructions cached in the TypeScript SDK, see <a href="#cache-the-static-part-of-a-custom-prompt" class="link">Cache the static part of a custom prompt</a>.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The following example pairs a shared <code>append</code> block with <code>excludeDynamicSections</code> so a fleet of agents can reuse the same cached system prompt:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <span data-as="p"><strong>Tradeoffs:</strong> the text that moves out of the system prompt still reaches Claude, but in a user message. That text is at least the auto memory directory’s location, and often the whole auto memory section. Instructions in a user message carry marginally less weight than the same text in the system prompt, so Claude may follow its auto memory guidance less consistently. Enable this option when cross-session cache reuse matters more than that.</span>
<span data-as="p">For the equivalent flag in non-interactive CLI mode, see <a href="/docs/en/cli-reference" class="link"><code>--exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections</code></a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="custom-system-prompts" aria-labelledby="_R_2p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#custom-system-prompts" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Custom system prompts"></a><span id="_R_2p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Custom system prompts</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">You can provide a custom string as <code>systemPrompt</code> to replace the default entirely with your own instructions.</span>

<span data-as="p">In Python, load a large custom prompt from a file with <code>system_prompt={&quot;type&quot;: &quot;file&quot;, &quot;path&quot;: &quot;...&quot;}</code> instead of passing it as a string. The Python SDK passes a string prompt as one command-line argument to the CLI subprocess, so a prompt that exceeds the OS argument-length limit fails at process spawn before any API request is sent. On Linux the error is <code>Argument list too long</code>. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#systempromptfile" class="link"><code>SystemPromptFile</code></a> for the platform thresholds and the Windows behavior.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="cache-the-static-part-of-a-custom-prompt" aria-labelledby="_R_319deehkd74t9_"><a href="#cache-the-static-part-of-a-custom-prompt" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Cache the static part of a custom prompt"></a><span id="_R_319deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Cache the static part of a custom prompt</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">In the TypeScript SDK, you can pass a custom prompt as an array of strings instead of one string, with the <code>SYSTEM_PROMPT_DYNAMIC_BOUNDARY</code> marker between the static part and the rest. Use this when your prompt combines instructions that are the same on every request with context that changes per request, such as the customer or ticket the agent is handling. When you pass both parts as one string, a change to the per-request part changes the whole system prompt, so the static instructions miss the cache too. The array form isn’t available in the Python SDK; <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions" class="link"><code>ClaudeAgentOptions</code></a> lists the forms <code>system_prompt</code> accepts.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">Claude Code splits the prompt only when it calls the Claude API directly or runs on <a href="/docs/en/claude-platform-on-aws" class="link">Claude Platform on AWS</a>. In every other configuration, such as Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, Microsoft Foundry, or an <a href="/docs/en/llm-gateway-connect" class="link">LLM gateway</a>, it sends the whole prompt as one block, the same as passing one string. The same happens whenever you set <a href="/docs/en/llm-gateway-protocol#disable-pre-release-capabilities" class="link"><code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_BETAS=1</code></a>.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To split the prompt, import <code>SYSTEM_PROMPT_DYNAMIC_BOUNDARY</code> from <code>@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk</code> and pass it as its own array element between the two parts. The SDK sends the strings before the marker as one text block and the strings after it as a second block, each with its own cache breakpoint. In the example below, a support agent loads its triage instructions from a file and receives details about one ticket on each request, so the instructions stay cached while the ticket details change:</span>

<span data-as="p"><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking#track-cache-tokens" class="link">Track cache tokens</a> describes the <code>cache_creation_input_tokens</code> and <code>cache_read_input_tokens</code> fields on each result message.</span>
<span data-as="p">The SDK assembles the blocks from the array as follows:</span>
<ul>
<li>The SDK joins the strings on each side of the marker with a blank line between them and removes the marker itself, so the marker text doesn’t reach Claude.</li>
<li>If you include the marker more than once, the first one is the split and the SDK removes the others.</li>
<li>If you leave the marker out, the SDK joins all the strings into one block, the same as passing one string.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">With the CLI’s <a href="/docs/en/cli-reference#system-prompt-flags" class="link"><code>--system-prompt</code> or <code>--system-prompt-file</code> flags</a>, the prompt is one string, so there is no array to carry the marker. Include a line containing only <code>__SYSTEM_PROMPT_DYNAMIC_BOUNDARY__</code> between the static and per-request parts instead. Claude Code splits the prompt at the first such line into the same two blocks and removes that line. Requires Claude Code v2.1.275 or later.</span>
<span data-as="p">In the SDK, prefer the array form, which carries the boundary without a marker line.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="change-the-prompt-of-an-existing-session" aria-labelledby="_R_3l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#change-the-prompt-of-an-existing-session" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Change the prompt of an existing session"></a><span id="_R_3l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Change the prompt of an existing session</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">By default, if you pass a different <code>append</code> or custom prompt when you return to a session with <code>resume</code> or <code>continue</code>, Claude doesn’t see it on the next turn. Claude Code records the system prompt on a session’s first request and reuses that record until the session is compacted. The new text takes effect after that compaction, or in a new session.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="update-claude’s-instructions-mid-session" aria-labelledby="_R_3p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#update-claude’s-instructions-mid-session" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Update Claude’s instructions mid-session"></a><span id="_R_3p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Update Claude’s instructions mid-session</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">If the instructions you put in the system prompt need to change while a session is running, for example because your user switched the agent to a read-only mode or edited its configuration in your app, send the new instructions in the conversation instead of changing <code>systemPrompt</code>:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>In your next message</strong>: include the new instructions in the next user message you send.</li>
<li><strong>From a hook</strong>: return <a href="/docs/en/hooks#add-context-for-claude" class="link"><code>additionalContext</code></a> from a <code>UserPromptSubmit</code> or <code>PostToolUse</code> <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks#outputs" class="link">hook callback</a>, written as a factual statement such as “The workspace is now read-only”. The SDK inserts the text into the conversation at the point where the hook fired, so the recorded prompt stays unchanged.</li>
</ul>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="turn-recording-off-while-you-iterate-on-wording" aria-labelledby="_R_3v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#turn-recording-off-while-you-iterate-on-wording" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Turn recording off while you iterate on wording"></a><span id="_R_3v9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Turn recording off while you iterate on wording</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">While you iterate on prompt wording and want each edit to reach a session you resume, set <code>snapshot</code> to false on the object form of the system prompt. Claude Code then rebuilds the prompt on every request. The field is available on the preset and custom forms of <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>systemPrompt</code></a> in TypeScript and of <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#systempromptpreset" class="link"><code>system_prompt</code></a> in Python, and requires <code>@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk</code> v0.3.257 or later, or <code>claude-agent-sdk</code> v0.2.153 or later.</span>
<span data-as="p">Keep recording on in production. With recording off, a different <code>append</code> or custom prompt on a resumed session reaches Claude on the next turn, and that request can’t reuse the session’s <a href="/docs/en/prompt-caching#how-the-cache-is-organized" class="link">prompt cache</a>. Where the API enforces <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/preserved-thinking" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">preserved thinking</a>, Claude also loses its thinking from earlier turns.</span>
<span data-as="p">Outside of <a href="/docs/en/cloud-environments" class="link">cloud sessions</a>, if you start Claude Code in <a href="/docs/en/headless#start-faster-with-bare-mode" class="link">bare mode</a> by passing <code>--bare</code> through <code>extraArgs</code> or setting <code>CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1</code>, recording stays off unless you set <code>snapshot: true</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">Recording an <code>append</code> or custom prompt by default requires Claude Code v2.1.265 or later, which the TypeScript Agent SDK bundles from v0.3.265 and the Python Agent SDK from v0.2.153. Before Claude Code v2.1.268, sessions that don’t <a href="/docs/en/env-vars#features-that-need-feature-flag-fetching" class="link">fetch feature flags</a>, including sessions on Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, and Microsoft Foundry, rebuilt the prompt on every request and <code>snapshot</code> had no effect.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="context-claude-code-adds-outside-the-system-prompt" aria-labelledby="_R_499deehkd74t9_"><a href="#context-claude-code-adds-outside-the-system-prompt" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Context Claude Code adds outside the system prompt"></a><span id="_R_499deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Context Claude Code adds outside the system prompt</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">System reminders are messages Claude Code adds to the conversation during a session to give Claude context, such as the contents of your CLAUDE.md files. Claude Code sends them in the conversation, not in the system prompt, so most reach Claude whether you use the <code>claude_code</code> preset or pass your own string as <code>systemPrompt</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">This section covers the <a href="#reminders-claude-code-adds-to-the-conversation" class="link">reminders most likely to change how your agent behaves</a>, how to <a href="#turn-off-the-context-your-agent-replaces" class="link">turn off the ones your agent replaces</a>, and how to <a href="#see-what-claude-received" class="link">see what Claude received</a> in a specific request.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="reminders-claude-code-adds-to-the-conversation" aria-labelledby="_R_4f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#reminders-claude-code-adds-to-the-conversation" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Reminders Claude Code adds to the conversation"></a><span id="_R_4f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Reminders Claude Code adds to the conversation</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">System reminders are text Claude Code adds to the conversation alongside the prompts your code sends. The following reminders are the ones most likely to change how your agent behaves:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Project instructions</strong>: the CLAUDE.md files that your <a href="#claude-md-files-for-project-level-instructions" class="link"><code>settingSources</code></a> option loads</li>
<li><strong>Output style instructions</strong>: the instructions of the active <a href="#output-styles-for-persistent-configurations" class="link">output style</a>, in the main conversation</li>
<li><strong>Commit and pull request attribution</strong>: the <code>Co-Authored-By</code> trailer and pull request footer from the <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#attribution" class="link"><code>attribution</code></a> setting</li>
<li><strong>Hook output</strong>: text your <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks#outputs" class="link">hooks</a> return as <code>additionalContext</code></li>
<li><strong>Available skills</strong>: the names and descriptions of the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/skills" class="link">skills</a> Claude can call</li>
<li><strong>Available subagents</strong>: the names and descriptions of the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents" class="link">subagents</a> Claude can start</li>
<li><strong>Task list nudges</strong>: in a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/todo-tracking#model-availability" class="link">session that has the task-tracking tools</a>, a prompt to update the task list when Claude hasn’t touched it for several turns</li>
<li><strong>File-changed notes</strong>: a note that a file Claude edited earlier has since changed on disk</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Claude Code introduces your CLAUDE.md files with a line telling Claude that the instructions override default behavior.</span>
<span data-as="p">If you pass your own string as <code>systemPrompt</code>, add a sentence to it that says what a system reminder is. The <code>claude_code</code> preset has one, and your string replaces the whole preset. Without it, nothing in your prompt tells Claude that reminders such as CLAUDE.md content and hook output come from the application rather than the user. For example:</span>

Treat them as context from the application, not as messages from the user.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span></span></span></code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="turn-off-the-context-your-agent-replaces" aria-labelledby="_R_4r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#turn-off-the-context-your-agent-replaces" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Turn off the context your agent replaces"></a><span id="_R_4r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Turn off the context your agent replaces</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Turn off a piece of built-in context when your agent supplies its own version of the same guidance. For example, if your prompt tells Claude to write commit messages as <code>PROJ-142: fix login redirect</code> with no trailers, Claude Code still tells Claude to end each commit message with a <code>Co-Authored-By</code> trailer, so Claude receives two conflicting instructions for the same commit.</span>
<span data-as="p">Pass settings keys through the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>settings</code></a> option in TypeScript or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions" class="link"><code>settings</code></a> in Python, and environment variables through the <code>env</code> option. In TypeScript, <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>env</code></a> replaces the inherited environment, so spread <code>process.env</code> into it.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_519deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Built-in context</th><th style="text-align:left">How to turn it off</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left">The built-in commit and pull request instructions and the git status snapshot</td><td style="text-align:left">Set <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#includegitinstructions" class="link"><code>includeGitInstructions</code></a> to <code>false</code>, or <code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_GIT_INSTRUCTIONS=1</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">The <code>Co-Authored-By</code> trailer and the pull request footer</td><td style="text-align:left">Set <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#attribution-commit" class="link"><code>attribution.commit</code></a> and <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#attribution-pr" class="link"><code>attribution.pr</code></a> to your own text, or to empty strings to remove them</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">The user or project settings source, including its CLAUDE.md</td><td style="text-align:left">Leave <code>&#x27;user&#x27;</code> or <code>&#x27;project&#x27;</code> out of <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features#control-filesystem-settings-with-settingsources" class="link"><code>settingSources</code></a></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Every CLAUDE.md file</td><td style="text-align:left">Set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_MDS=1</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Task list nudges, file-changed notes, and the skill list</td><td style="text-align:left">Set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ATTACHMENTS=1</code></td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Set <code>includeGitInstructions</code> to <code>false</code> to keep out the built-in commit and pull request instructions, which are part of the Bash tool’s description and reach Claude even with a custom <code>systemPrompt</code>. With the minimal default, a custom <code>systemPrompt</code>, or a custom main-thread agent that has its own prompt, though, the session starts without the git status snapshot.</span>
<ul>
<li>If you set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ATTACHMENTS</code>, Claude Code also sends <code>@</code> file mentions as plain text instead of expanding them into file content. The list of available subagents and background task notifications still arrive.</li>
<li>To stop only <code>@</code> file mentions from expanding, set <a href="/docs/en/env-vars#variables" class="link"><code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FILE_MENTIONS=1</code></a> instead, on Claude Code v2.1.299 or later. Claude then gets the mentioned paths plus a note to read them with its file tools, and your <code>PreToolUse</code> hooks see each file read as a tool call.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The following example is for an agent that carries its own commit rules in <code>append</code>. It sets both <code>attribution</code> keys to empty strings to remove the trailer and footer, and turns off <code>includeGitInstructions</code> so Claude Code’s own commit workflow instructions don’t compete with yours:</span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">                &quot;append&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">: </span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Write commit messages as: &lt;ticket id&gt;: &lt;summary&gt;. Add no trailers.&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">,</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">            },</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">            settings</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">=</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&#x27;{&quot;includeGitInstructions&quot;: false, &quot;attribution&quot;: {&quot;commit&quot;: &quot;&quot;, &quot;pr&quot;: &quot;&quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">}}</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&#x27;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">,</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">            allowed_tools</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">=</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">[</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Bash(git *)&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">],</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">        ),</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">    ):</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">        print</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(message)</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">asyncio.run(main())</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To confirm the change, run the example in a repository with staged changes and check the new commit with <code>git log -1</code>. The message ends without a <code>Co-Authored-By</code> trailer.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="see-what-claude-received" aria-labelledby="_R_5d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#see-what-claude-received" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: See what Claude received"></a><span id="_R_5d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">See what Claude received</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The SDK message stream doesn’t include system reminders, so reading the messages your code receives won’t show you what Claude saw. To see them, log the requests Claude Code sends:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Raw request logging</strong>: set <a href="/docs/en/monitoring-usage#api-request-body-event" class="link"><code>OTEL_LOG_RAW_API_BODIES</code></a> to <code>file:&lt;dir&gt;</code>. Claude Code writes each request body to that directory.</li>
<li><strong>A gateway you control</strong>: point <a href="/docs/en/llm-gateway" class="link"><code>ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL</code></a> at a proxy that logs request bodies.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">In a logged request, look in the <code>messages</code> array. A reminder appears inside a user message wrapped in <code>&lt;system-reminder&gt;</code> tags or, on some models, as a separate message with the <code>system</code> role.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="compare-the-four-approaches" aria-labelledby="_R_5l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#compare-the-four-approaches" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Compare the four approaches"></a><span id="_R_5l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Compare the four approaches</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The four customization methods differ in where they live, how they’re shared, and what they preserve from the <code>claude_code</code> preset.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_5p9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Feature</th><th>CLAUDE.md</th><th>Output Styles</th><th><code>systemPrompt</code> with append</th><th>Custom <code>systemPrompt</code></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>Persistence</strong></td><td>Per-project file</td><td>Saved as files</td><td>Session only</td><td>Session only</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Reusability</strong></td><td>Per-project</td><td>Across projects</td><td>Code duplication</td><td>Code duplication</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Management</strong></td><td>On filesystem</td><td>CLI + files</td><td>In code</td><td>In code</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Tool guidance</strong></td><td>Preserved</td><td>Preserved</td><td>Preserved</td><td>Lost (unless included)</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Built-in safety</strong></td><td>Maintained</td><td>Maintained</td><td>Maintained</td><td>Must be added</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Customization level</strong></td><td>Additions only</td><td>Additions; can omit coding instructions</td><td>Additions only</td><td>Complete control</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Version control</strong></td><td>With project</td><td>Yes</td><td>With code</td><td>With code</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Scope</strong></td><td>Project-specific</td><td>User or project</td><td>Code session</td><td>Code session</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">“With append” means using <code>systemPrompt: { type: &quot;preset&quot;, preset: &quot;claude_code&quot;, append: &quot;...&quot; }</code> in TypeScript or <code>system_prompt={&quot;type&quot;: &quot;preset&quot;, &quot;preset&quot;: &quot;claude_code&quot;, &quot;append&quot;: &quot;...&quot;}</code> in Python. CLAUDE.md doesn’t change the system prompt itself: the SDK injects its content into the conversation as project context.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="combine-approaches" aria-labelledby="_R_5t9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#combine-approaches" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Combine approaches"></a><span id="_R_5t9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Combine approaches</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The approaches compose. A persistent output style or CLAUDE.md sets the long-lived behavior, and <code>append</code> layers session-specific instructions on top without touching the saved configuration.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="combine-an-output-style-with-session-specific-additions" aria-labelledby="_R_619deehkd74t9_"><a href="#combine-an-output-style-with-session-specific-additions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Combine an output style with session-specific additions"></a><span id="_R_619deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Combine an output style with session-specific additions</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The example below assumes a Code Reviewer output style is already active. The <code>append</code> block layers session-specific focus areas on top of the persona, so a single review session can prioritize OAuth and token storage without changing the saved output style:</span>

0.4)}\u0026:active{background-color:hsl(var(--bg-400))}}\u0026.underline{opacity:0.8;text-decoration-line:none;text-underline-offset:3px;transition:all This differs from `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude -p`}),`, which uses the Claude Code system prompt by default. If you’re migrating from the CLI and want matching behavior, set the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset`]}),`: the system prompt that the Claude Code CLI uses, with tool usage instructions and security and safety instructions. Set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`systemPrompt: { type: \"preset\", preset: \"claude_code\" }`}),` in TypeScript or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`system_prompt={\"type\": \"preset\", \"preset\": \"claude_code\"}`}),` in Python, optionally with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`append`}),` to add your own instructions on the end.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Custom string`}),`: a prompt you write yourself. The SDK sends only what you provide.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`decide-on-a-starting-point`,children:`Decide on a starting point`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`The deciding factor is how closely your agent resembles Claude Code: a coding agent operating in a repository, with a human watching streaming output and steering the work. The further your product is from that, the more you’ll want to write your own prompt.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(Table,{children:[_jsx(_components.thead,{children:_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.th,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`You’re building`}),_jsx(_components.th,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Use`}),_jsx(_components.th,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`What you get`})]})}),_jsxs(_components.tbody,{children:[_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`A CLI or IDE-like coding tool where a human watches and steers, and Claude Code’s defaults are what you want`}),_jsxs(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset`]}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`The Claude Code prompt, including tool guidance and safety rules`})]}),_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`The same kind of tool, plus product-specific rules like coding standards, output format, or domain context`}),_jsxs(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`append`})]}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Everything above, with your instructions added after the preset. Nothing is removed, so this is the lowest-risk customization`})]}),_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`An agent with a different surface, identity, or permission model, or a non-coding agent`}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Custom prompt string`}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Only what you write. You take responsibility for replacing the tool guidance and safety instructions your agent still needs`})]}),_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`A thin tool-calling loop with no agent persona, where you supply all behavior in the user prompt`}),_jsxs(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:[`No `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`systemPrompt`}),` option`]}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`The minimal default: tool-calling support and nothing else`})]})]})]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`“Different from Claude Code” usually means one of the following:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Different surface`}),`: the output isn’t read in a terminal by the person who triggered it. Chat UIs, structured-output consumers, and non-coding automation each need a prompt that matches how their output is rendered and reviewed. Unattended coding automation, like a CI job that fixes lint errors or reviews diffs, still fits the preset because the work itself is what the preset is written for.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Different identity`}),`: the agent shouldn’t present itself as Claude Code. A support bot, a data-analysis assistant, or any domain-specific agent needs its own name, scope, and persona.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Different permission model`}),`: the agent runs autonomously without a human approving each step, or operates on a narrow set of resources. Claude Code’s prompt assumes a human is in the loop with access to a full toolset.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Non-coding tasks`}),`: most of Claude Code’s prompt is coding guidance. For research, content, or operations agents, that guidance competes with the instructions you actually need.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#compare-the-four-approaches`,children:`comparison table`}),` shows what each customization method preserves.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`customize-agent-behavior`,children:`Customize agent behavior`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`append`}),` and a custom prompt string each change the system prompt directly, and an output style changes the instructions Claude Code gives Claude for every response. CLAUDE.md takes a different path: the SDK reads it and injects its content into the conversation as project context, so it shapes behavior alongside whichever system prompt you choose. `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/skills`,children:`Skills`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/hooks`,children:`hooks`}),`, and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/permissions`,children:`permissions`}),` also shape behavior outside the system prompt and are covered on their own pages.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`claude-md-files-for-project-level-instructions`,children:`CLAUDE.md files for project-level instructions`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`CLAUDE.md files give Claude persistent project context and instructions. The SDK injects their content into the conversation and leaves the system prompt untouched, so they work with any system prompt configuration. For what to put in CLAUDE.md, where to place it, and how to write effective instructions, see `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/memory#when-to-add-to-claude-md`,children:`When to add to CLAUDE.md`}),` and the rest of `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/memory`,children:`How Claude remembers your project`}),`. This section covers what’s specific to the SDK: how CLAUDE.md loads.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The SDK reads CLAUDE.md when the matching setting source is enabled: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`'project'`}),` loads `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE.md`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`.claude/CLAUDE.md`}),` from the working directory, and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`'user'`}),` loads `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`}),`. Default `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` options enable both sources, so CLAUDE.md loads automatically. If you set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`settingSources`}),` in TypeScript or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`setting_sources`}),` in Python explicitly, include the sources you need. CLAUDE.md loading is controlled by setting sources, not by the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`load-claude-md-with-the-sdk`,children:`Load CLAUDE.md with the SDK`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To load CLAUDE.md, set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`settingSources`}),` to include the level where you keep your CLAUDE.md. init message, assistant messages, user messages carrying tool results, and a final result message with the session outcome.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`CLAUDE.md is persistent across all sessions in a project, shared with your team through git, and discovered automatically without code changes. It is not loaded if you pass an empty `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`settingSources`}),` array.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`output-styles-for-persistent-configurations`,children:`Output styles for persistent configurations`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Output styles are saved sets of instructions that change Claude’s role, tone, and output format. They’re stored as markdown files and can be reused across sessions and projects.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`create-an-output-style`,children:`Create an output style`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`An output style is a markdown file with `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/output-styles#frontmatter`,children:`frontmatter`}),` for metadata, followed by the prompt content. Save it to `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`~/.claude/output-styles/`}),` for a user-level style available in every project, or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`.claude/output-styles/`}),` in your repository for a project-level style you can commit and share with your team.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`A custom output style leaves the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset’s software engineering instructions out and uses your own. To keep them and layer your instructions on top, set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`keep-coding-instructions: true`}),` in the frontmatter. Those instructions are only in Claude Code’s full system prompt, so the setting has no effect in a session on the shorter system prompt; set `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/env-vars#variables`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT`})}),` to `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`0`}),` to select the full prompt on any model. Keep them when your agent is still doing software engineering work. Python, load a large custom prompt from a file with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`system_prompt={\"type\": \"file\", \"path\": \"...\"}`}),` instead of passing it as a string. The Python SDK passes a string prompt as one command-line argument to the CLI subprocess, so a prompt that exceeds the OS argument-length limit fails at process spawn before any API request is sent. On Linux the error is `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Argument list too long`}),`. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#systempromptfile`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SystemPromptFile`})}),` for the platform thresholds and the Windows behavior.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`cache-the-static-part-of-a-custom-prompt`,children:`Cache the static part of a custom prompt`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In the TypeScript SDK, you can pass a custom prompt as an array of strings instead of one string, with the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SYSTEM_PROMPT_DYNAMIC_BOUNDARY`}),` marker between the static part and the rest. Use this when your prompt combines instructions that are the same on every request with context that changes per request, such as the customer or ticket the agent is handling. When you pass both parts as one string, a change to the per-request part changes the whole system prompt, so the static instructions miss the cache too. The array form isn’t available in the Python SDK; `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ClaudeAgentOptions`})}),` lists the forms `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`system_prompt`}),` accepts.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Note,{children:_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Claude Code splits the prompt only when it calls the Claude API directly or runs on `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/claude-platform-on-aws`,children:`Claude Platform on AWS`}),`. In every other configuration, such as Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, Microsoft Foundry, or an `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/llm-gateway-connect`,children:`LLM gateway`}),`, it sends the whole prompt as one block, the same as passing one string. The same happens whenever you set `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/llm-gateway-protocol#disable-pre-release-capabilities`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_BETAS=1`})}),`.`]})}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To split the prompt, import `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SYSTEM_PROMPT_DYNAMIC_BOUNDARY`}),` from `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`}),` and pass it as its own array element between the two parts. The SDK sends the strings before the marker as one text block and the strings after it as a second block, each with its own cache breakpoint. In the example below, a support agent loads its triage instructions from a file and receives details about one ticket on each request, so the instructions stay cached while the ticket details Requires Claude Code v2.1.275 or later.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`In the SDK, prefer the array form, which carries the boundary without a marker line.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`change-the-prompt-of-an-existing-session`,children:`Change the prompt of an existing session`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`By default, if you pass a different `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`append`}),` or custom prompt when you return to a session with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`resume`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`continue`}),`, Claude doesn’t see it on the next turn. Claude Code records the system prompt on a session’s first request and reuses that record until the session is compacted. The new text takes effect after that compaction, or in a new session.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`update-claude’s-instructions-mid-session`,children:`Update Claude’s instructions mid-session`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`If the instructions you put in the system prompt need to change while a session is running, for example because your user switched the agent to a read-only mode or edited its configuration in your app, send the new instructions in the conversation instead of changing `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`systemPrompt`}),`:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`In your next message`}),`: include the new instructions in the next user message you send.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`From a hook`}),`: return `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/hooks#add-context-for-claude`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`additionalContext`})}),` from a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`UserPromptSubmit`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PostToolUse`}),` `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/hooks#outputs`,children:`hook callback`}),`, written as a factual statement such as “The workspace is now read-only”. The SDK inserts the text into the conversation at the point where the hook fired, so the recorded prompt stays unchanged.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`turn-recording-off-while-you-iterate-on-wording`,children:`Turn recording off while you iterate on wording`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`While you iterate on prompt wording and want each edit to reach a session you resume, set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`snapshot`}),` to false on the object form of the system prompt. Claude Code then rebuilds the prompt on every request. The field is available on the preset and custom forms of `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`systemPrompt`})}),` in TypeScript and of `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#systempromptpreset`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`system_prompt`})}),` in Python, and requires `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`}),` v0.3.257 or later, or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude-agent-sdk`}),` v0.2.153 or later.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Keep recording on in production. With recording off, a different `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`append`}),` or custom prompt on a resumed session reaches Claude on the next turn, and that request can’t reuse the session’s `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/prompt-caching#how-the-cache-is-organized`,children:`prompt cache`}),`. Where the API enforces `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/preserved-thinking`,children:`preserved thinking`}),`, Claude also loses its thinking from earlier turns.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Outside of `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/cloud-environments`,children:`cloud sessions`}),`, if you start Claude Code in `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/headless#start-faster-with-bare-mode`,children:`bare mode`}),` by passing `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`--bare`}),` through `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`extraArgs`}),` or setting `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1`}),`, recording stays off unless you set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`snapshot: true`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Recording an `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`append`}),` or custom prompt by default requires Claude Code v2.1.265 or later, which the TypeScript Agent SDK bundles from v0.3.265 and the Python Agent SDK from v0.2.153. Before Claude Code v2.1.268, sessions that don’t `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/env-vars#features-that-need-feature-flag-fetching`,children:`fetch feature flags`}),`, including sessions on Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, and Microsoft Foundry, rebuilt the prompt on every request and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`snapshot`}),` had no effect.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`context-claude-code-adds-outside-the-system-prompt`,children:`Context Claude Code adds outside the system prompt`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`System reminders are messages Claude Code adds to the conversation during a session to give Claude context, such as the contents of your CLAUDE.md files. Claude Code sends them in the conversation, not in the system prompt, so most reach Claude whether you use the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude_code`}),` preset or pass your own string as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`systemPrompt`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`This section covers the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#reminders-claude-code-adds-to-the-conversation`,children:`reminders most likely to change how your agent behaves`}),`, how to `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#turn-off-the-context-your-agent-replaces`,children:`turn off the ones your agent replaces`}),`, and how to `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#see-what-claude-received`,children:`see what Claude received`}),` in a specific request.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`reminders-claude-code-adds-to-the-conversation`,children:`Reminders Claude Code adds to the conversation`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`System reminders are text Claude Code adds to the conversation alongside the prompts your code sends.
